- **reasoning-techniques**: Structured reasoning for software developers and coding agents. - **reasoning-templates**: Execute the PAG reasoning protocols in .{provider}/templates/ when producing a plan, checklist, task breakdown, workflow, agent, or verification flow. - **reasoning-templates**: When delegating a sub-task to an agent or drafting a prompt and the user wants a proven structure instead of an ad-hoc one — "write me a prompt for this", "structure this request", "which prompt pattern should I use", spawning a sub-agent, or setting up a role for research, tool-driven work, or expert output. - **reasoning-tilt**: Use when you want to detect whether a session's command and output text is leaning toward forward-looking framing ("next", "let me try", "plan to") vs. history-following framing ("blocked", "failed", "can't"). Scores a JSONL trace file with a forward/history lexical ratio and flags sessions that have drifted into the cursed regime (ratio &lt; 0.40). - **reasoning-transparency**: Reasoning visibility and logic-surfacing protocol. Trigger explicitly when Myra asks 'why', 'how did you get there', 'walk me through your thinking', or 'explain your logic'. - **reasoning-tree-of-thoughts**: Use when a problem has several genuinely different candidate approaches and an early wrong turn is expensive or hard to undo — planning, puzzles, architecture/strategy trade-offs, combinatorial decisions, scheduling, multi-criteria choices. Searches a tree of partial solutions, scores each branch with a cheap evaluator, prunes the weak ones, expands only survivors, and can backtrack.
